⏺ T4 is done and committed locally on the new branch milestone-b-audience,
  created from main. Nothing is pushed, and I've stopped before T5.

  Your decision: an empty rule list is now rejected and never means "everyone".
  It's recorded in D14 with a dated note.

  What T4 adds (internal/audience)
  - The rule catalog from D14:
    - city
    - cart item added within a window
    - no order within a window
    - order count (at least / at most)
    - total spend (at least / at most)
    - last order before or after a date
    - category ordered or carted within a window
    - cart value
    - signed up within a window
    - language, which is rejected as reserved
  - Time windows are either "last N days", counted from when the rule is
    evaluated, or fixed dates resolved in the campaign's time zone. The "to"
    date includes the whole day.
  - Validation applies every limit from the plan: 1–365 days, valid dates from
    2000-01-01 with "from" not after "to", amounts 0–1,000,000, 1–20 rules, no
    repeated field and operator pair, and unknown JSON keys rejected. It reports
    every bad rule at once. City names are matched loosely (Istanbul, ISTANBUL
    → İstanbul), as are category names.
  - SQL: each field has its own fixed query fragment, and rule values only ever
    travel to Postgres as $n arguments, never as SQL text.
  - Consent: building an audience always requires a channel, so consent can't be
    skipped. Count returns per-channel counts plus a total that counts each
    user once.

  Result: all 6 packages pass; 13 new audience tests

  Result: 11 users, one per scenario. 14 rule cases compare the exact list of

  Commits (local only)
  - a9a586f feat: add audience rule validation and parameterized SQL builder
  - e815f55 docs: require at least one audience rule (D14) and update README
  - e84cad5 docs: add session export 04 (milestone A). I scanned it first: the
    GitHub token appears only masked, and the only email is the demo login.

  Choices I made
  - The plan's signatures needed adjusting:
    - Validate takes the catalog and returns the corrected rules.
    - Build takes the time and time zone, requires a channel, and returns an
      error.
  - Value formats: cities as a plain list (["İstanbul"]), windows as
    {"last_days": 30} or {"from": …, "to": …}, categories as {"categories": 
    [...], …window}. T5 will teach Claude this format.
  - "Last order before a date" means the user's most recent order is older than
    that date. Users who never ordered don't match.
  - Note for T6: the api's runtime image has no time-zone database, so T6 must
    embed Go's time/tzdata. It's in my progress log.

  Next, your call: should I push milestone-b-audience to GitHub? I won't start
  T5 until you approve it.

⏺ T5 is done and committed locally on milestone-b-audience. As you asked, I

  What T5 adds
  - Mock AI (LLM_MODE=mock): it understands 8 brief patterns in both English and
    Turkish (16 example briefs), now listed in the README. A brief may also end
    with one tone sentence and one send-time sentence ("tomorrow at 10:00",
    "yarın saat 10:00'da gönder", "in 2 hours").
    - It answers "unsupported" for anything else: an unknown brief, an extra
      condition ("…who like hiking"), an unknown city or category, an
      out-of-range number, or two send times.
    - It never guesses or broadens an audience.
  - Real Claude (LLM_MODE=real):
    - It uses claude-opus-5-5 with JSON output forced into a schema, adaptive
      thinking, and high effort, since a misread audience means messages go to
      the wrong people.
    - The prompt includes the current time in the campaign's time zone, the city
      and category lists, and the brief marked as data, not instructions. It
      never includes user records.
    - The prompt also tells Claude to answer "unsupported" rather than drop,
      approximate or add conditions.
  - campaign.Interpret (the D15 rules):
    - Brief length is checked before any AI call.
    - Every answer is validated, and an invalid one is retried once with the
      errors. After a second failure the request fails and nothing is saved.
    - API errors and timeouts are not retried, and each call has its own
      timeout.
    - A suggested send time in the past or more than 30 days ahead is not
      prefilled; a note says why, and the rules are kept.

  Choices I made

    the mock and the real AI pass through exactly the same checks.

    force Claude to pick a "valid" city even when the brief says Paris; with

✻ Cooked for 29s · done 3:45 PM

❯ is 1,000 characters enough for the campaign brief? what happens if the limit  
  is exceed                                                                     

⏺ Yes. 1,000 characters is enough for a campaign brief: the longest example
  brief we have uses less than a fifth of it.

  How much room it gives
  - The CASE.md example brief is about 190 characters. Its Turkish version is
    about 170.
  - 1,000 characters is roughly 150–180 words, or 6–10 sentences. That covers an
    audience description, the offer, a tone instruction and a send time with a
    lot of room left.
  - A brief describes who gets the message and what it's about. The message text
    itself is written later (T8) and has its own limits: 160 characters for
    SMS, 2,000 for an email body.
  - Turkish characters (ş, ğ, İ) count as one character each, the same counting
    as the SMS limit (D2).

  What happens if a brief is too long
  - Now (T5): campaign.Interpret trims leading and trailing spaces, counts the
    characters, and returns the error "the request must be 1 to 1000 characters"
    right away.
    - The AI is never called, so no cost and no waiting.
    - Nothing is cut off: the brief is never truncated and sent in pieces,
      because the dropped part could hold an audience condition.
    - Nothing is saved.
    - Tests cover 1,000 characters accepted, 1,001 rejected, and empty or
      spaces-only rejected.
  - From T6: POST /api/campaigns will return 422 with that message, and the
    marketer can shorten the brief and send it again.
  - From T16 (the brief input screen): I'd add a live character counter ("640 /
    1000") that blocks sending past the limit, so the error rarely shows. The
    server check stays as the real guard.

  Why have a limit at all
  Claude itself could read far more. The limit only protects the project:
  - It keeps AI cost per request predictable.
  - It reduces abuse, such as pasting a whole document.
  - It gives less room for instructions hidden in the brief.

  My recommendation: approve 1,000. If marketers will paste extra context, such
  as product details or a previous campaign, 2,000 is equally safe; changing it
  is one constant plus one test value. I'd avoid going much higher, because a
  long brief tends to mix several audiences, which leads to more "unsupported"
  answers.

  Should I record 1,000 (or 2,000) as approved in DECISIONS.md?

      38  
      39  Every `/api/*` endpoint except `POST /api/login` returns 401 wi
          thout a session. Sessions last 12 hours and live in an HttpOnly
          , SameSite=Lax cookie; the database stores only the token's SHA
          -256 hash. The cookie is not marked `Secure` because the demo r
          uns on plain `http://localhost`.
      40  
      41 +## Campaign draft API                                          
      42 +                                                               
      43 +All endpoints need a login session (see above).                
      44 +                                                               
      45 +| Endpoint | Purpose |                                         
      46 +|---|---|                                                      
      47 +| `POST /api/campaigns` `{"request", "channels", "time_zone"}` 
         +| The AI reads the brief and a **draft** is saved with its audi
         +ence rules, current per-channel counts, and a proposed send tim
         +e if the brief named one (only a proposal; nothing is scheduled
         +). Add `"rules": [...]` to build the audience by hand without t
         +he AI. |                                                       
      48 +| `PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/rules` `{"rules", "version"}` | Repl
         +ace the draft's rules; they are validated and recounted. |     
      49 +| `PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/schedule` `{"send_at", "version"}` |
         + Change or clear (`null`) the proposed send time: RFC 3339 with
         + offset, between now and 30 days ahead. |                      
      50 +| `GET /api/campaigns`, `GET /api/campaigns/{id}` | List (newes
         +t 200) / one campaign; drafts include current counts. |        
      51 +                                                               
      52 +`channels` is `["sms"]`, `["email"]` or both; `time_zone` is th
         +e browser's IANA zone (e.g. `Europe/Istanbul`). Edits send the 
         +`version` they were based on; a stale version or a non-draft ca
         +mpaign returns 409. Invalid input returns 422 (with `field_erro
         +rs` for rules); an "unsupported" brief returns 422 with `"unsup
         +ported": true` and the AI's reason, and nothing is saved. If th
         +e AI answers invalidly twice, times out or is unavailable, the 
         +API returns 502/504 and nothing is saved.                      
      53 +                                                               
      54 +```sh                                                          
      55 +curl -b cookies.txt -X POST localhost:8080/api/campaigns -d '{"
         +request":"Send a weekend sale announcement to all users in Anka
         +ra. Send it tomorrow at 10:00.","channels":["sms"],"time_zone":
         +"Europe/Istanbul"}'                                            
      56 +```                                                            
      57 +

         +ODE=real` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.env`, run `docker compos
         +e up --build`, log in as above, and post a free-form brief (e.g
         +. "Send a thank-you note to customers in Izmir who spent at lea
         +st 2,000 TL"). Expect a draft with matching rules, or `"unsuppo
         +rted": true` with a reason for a brief the rule catalog can't e
         +xpress (e.g. one that mentions age). `LLM_TIMEOUT` (default `60
         +s`) limits each AI call. Each call costs real API credits.

           extra condition, is answered as **unsupported**: the mock neve
          r guesses or broadens an audience (D15). For free-form briefs,

⏺ T6 (the campaign draft API) is done and committed locally on
  milestone-b-audience; I haven't pushed it yet. That completes Milestone B
  (T4–T6). I paused the Docker investigation as you asked: no processes are
  hanging, and the main cause is that the test container keeps no build cache.

  What T6 adds

  Endpoint: POST /api/campaigns
  What it does: The AI (mock or Claude) reads the brief and a draft is saved
  with
  its rules, current counts per channel, and a proposed send time if the brief
  named one. The time is only a proposal: nothing is scheduled.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Endpoint: PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/rules
  What it does: Replaces the rules; they're validated and recounted.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Endpoint: PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/schedule
  What it does: Changes or clears the proposed time (between now and 30 days
  ahead).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Endpoint: GET /api/campaigns, GET /api/campaigns/{id}
  What it does: List, or one campaign; drafts include live counts.

  Error handling:
  - Bad input gets 422, with per-rule errors where relevant. That covers
    channels, time zone, brief length and rules.
  - An "unsupported" brief gets 422 with the AI's reason, and nothing is saved.
  - An AI that answers invalidly twice or is unavailable gets 502; a timeout
    gets 504. Nothing is saved, and the raw AI answer is never shown.
  - Every edit checks the version it was based on. A stale version, or a
    campaign that isn't a draft, gets 409.

  Carried over from T5 and now done: the AI is chosen by LLM_MODE, there's a
  configurable LLM_TIMEOUT (default 60 s), the time-zone database is embedded so
  the alpine image can resolve Europe/Istanbul, and the README has the manual
  real-AI check steps.

  Commits (local only)
  - 43ecb43 feat: add campaign draft API with AI or manual rules
  - b93b490 docs: document campaign draft API and manual real-AI check

  Choices I made
  - Rules built by hand: POST also accepts rules and then skips the AI, as D15
    promises ("build the rules by hand"); without it an unsupported brief would
    leave nothing to edit. Hand-built rules go through the same validation.
  - Suggested time replaced: when the marketer sets the send time, the brief's
    schedule note is cleared, since it no longer describes the chosen time.
  - AI channel suggestions are ignored: the marketer always chooses the channels
    (D1).

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Milestone B review (T4–T6), PR #2, main..dede8aa

  I read all the Go and SQL in the diff in one pass. I also ran a probe test
  against the mock, using a copy of HEAD in the scratchpad (repo untouched).
  `make test` passed in every package and `make lint` is clean; both ran for
  real.

  ### What's done well
  - **SQL builder** (`internal/audience/sql.go`): column names, operators and
  the `set` clause in `campaign/http.go:175,205` are all constants. Every value
  reaches Postgres as a `$n` argument. AI-generated SQL is never run.
  - **Consent**: `Build` requires a channel, and `Count` applies a per-channel
  opt-in filter. These are the only exported paths that select recipients.
  - **Turkish names**: `Istanbul`, `istanbul` and `ISTANBUL` all fold to the
  stored spelling `İstanbul` (`rules.go:309-315`, confirmed by probe).
  Categories are case-folded. An unknown name is a validation error, never a
  silent 0.
  - **Time windows**: fixed dates resolve in the campaign time zone, `to`
  includes the whole day, and a `from` date never slides.
  - **AI output (D15)**: strict decode, one retry with the errors, nothing saved
  on failure, generic error messages to the client, brief fenced as data, no
  user records in the prompt.
  - **Draft API**: optimistic locking with `WHERE version AND status='draft'`,
  64 KB body cap, unknown fields rejected, IANA zone check (also rejects `""`
  and `Local`).
  - **Build caches** are safe: cache mounts never land in the image, and `make
  test` only removes `test-db`.
  - **Secrets**: none in the branch. The session export only holds a masked
  `gho_****` mention, the demo password and the path `/Users/[user]`.

  ### Critical
  **1. The mock's offer slot lets audience conditions through, so they are
  silently dropped or broadened (breaks D15 and the "never guess" rule).**
  - **Where:** `internal/llm/mock.go:68` (`{offer}` = `.{1,100}?`) and `:152`
  (the `audienceWords` list is too narrow).
  - **Confirmed by probe:**
    - "Send a discount to everyone in Izmir and to users in Istanbul." → `city
  in [İstanbul]` (Izmir dropped).
    - "Ankara'daki tüm kullanıcılara 30 gündür sipariş vermeyenler için indirim
  gönder." → all Ankara users (the 30-day no-order condition is dropped).
    - "Ankara'daki tüm kullanıcılara sadece kadınlar için indirim gönder." → all
  Ankara users.
    - "Send a 20% discount valid only for women to users in istanbul." → all
  İstanbul users.
    - "Send a coupon except for VIPs to customers whose last order was more than
  90 days ago." → `last_order before 90`, with the exclusion dropped.
  - **Smallest fix:** narrow the offer slot to a short phrase, e.g.
  `(?:%?\d{1,2}%? )?[\pL'’-]+(?: [\pL'’-]+){0,3}` (at most 4 words, no commas).
  Also reject a small stop-word set inside it: for, to, in, only, except, and,
  or, with, without, not, için, sadece, hariç, ve, veya, ile. Add the five
  briefs above to `TestMock_ExtraCondition_Unsupported`. All documented examples
  still fit.

  ### Important
  **2. The SDK retries API errors and timeouts twice in production, but D15 says
  no retry.**
  - **Where:** `cmd/scc/main.go:83` builds the client with
  `llm.NewAnthropic(key, model)` and no options. anthropic-sdk-go v1.84.0
  defaults to `MaxRetries: 2` (`internal/requestconfig/requestconfig.go:175`).
  - **Scenario:** a 529, 500 or connection reset → up to 3 HTTP calls for one
  attempt. Combined with the validation retry, that is up to 6 Claude calls, all
  inside one 60 s window, so the 504 also arrives later than it should. The
  test hides this because `anthropic_test.go:49` passes
  `option.WithMaxRetries(0)` explicitly.
  - **Fix:** put `option.WithMaxRetries(0)` in `NewAnthropic`'s default options
  (before the caller's options), not only in the test.

  ### Minor
  **3. A wrong UTC offset from Claude shifts the prefilled send time silently.**
  - **Where:** `campaign/interpret.go:139`.
  - **Scenario:** "tomorrow at 10:00" in Europe/Istanbul comes back as
  `…T10:00:00Z` → prefilled 13:00 local. The marketer still sees it before
  approval.
  - **Fix:** if the offset of `at` differs from `at.In(loc)`'s offset, add a
  problem. That triggers the existing retry.

  **4. Two rules with the same field and operator are rejected, but the prompt
  doesn't say so.**
  - **Where:** `audience/rules.go:74-78`.
  - **Scenario:** "bought books in the last 30 days and electronics in the last
  90 days" → Claude returns two `ordered_category in` rules → duplicate error
  twice → 502 "invalid answer twice". The marketer should get "unsupported" or a
  valid audience.
  - **Fix:** one line in `interpretSystemPrompt`: each field and operator may
  appear at most once; otherwise answer unsupported.

  **5. "In 0 hours" schedules for right now.**
  - **Where:** `llm/mock.go:158`.
  - **Scenario:** "Send it in 0 hours" → `send_at = now`, which is accepted
  because `Before(now)` is false. The approval rule in T9 (at least 1 minute
  ahead) will catch it later.
  - **Fix:** use `[1-9]\d?` for the hours.

  **6. Channels are still requested from Claude, then thrown away.**
  - **Where:** `interpret.go:115-121`, plus the schema and prompt.
  - **Scenario:** a bad channel value costs a retry for a field nobody uses, and
  the request carries extra tokens.
  - **Fix (simplification, optional):** remove `channels` from the schema,
  prompt and `Interpretation`.

  ### Declined to judge
  - **"Yesterday at 10" or "next month" in mock mode:** the mock answers
  "unsupported" for the whole brief, so there is no note-only draft. Nothing
  gets scheduled, which meets the rule. In real mode, the past/>30-day note path
  in `usableSendAt` looks correct, but I couldn't run it without an API key.
  - **Prompt-injection fence:** a brief containing `</brief>` can close the
  fence early. The brief comes from the logged-in marketer, and the output is
  validated anyway, so I didn't count it as a finding.
  - **60 s timeout with adaptive thinking at high effort:** whether that is long
  enough can only be judged with real calls.
  - **Session export:** I scanned it with patterns for API keys, tokens and
  personal data, but did not read all 2,500 lines.

  ### Rulings
  I agree with all the Task 4–6 rulings:
  - Task 5, refusal fallback: superseded by the user's decision, and the code
  now uses only `client.Messages.New` with no beta leftovers.
  - Task 5, 1,000-character limit: approved in D15.
  - Task 6, optional manual rules: justified by D15.
  - Task 6, 502/504/422 mapping: reasonable.

  One caveat on Task 5's "free strings, no enum": it relies on Claude choosing
  "unsupported" for unknown names; otherwise the result is a 502 after the
  retry. That is acceptable.

  ### Verdict
  **Ready to merge: with fixes.** #1 is required: the mock breaks the D15 rule
  that conditions are never dropped. #2 is a one-line fix that brings real mode
  in line with D15. The rest can wait.

  What I did not check: real Claude calls, and a line-by-line read of the
  frontend-free README changes. My probe test lives only in the scratchpad copy.


⏺ Before reporting, I checked the two most serious findings myself rather than
  taking the reviewer's word for them:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Agent "Review Milestone B branch" finished · 4m 37s

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The independent review of PR #2 says don't merge yet: it found one critical
  and one important issue, and I re-checked both myself. It also found four
  minor issues. I haven't changed anything; the PR is still open and not merged.

  What held up
  - Rule values reach Postgres only as query arguments, never as SQL text, and
    no SQL written by the AI is ever run.
  - Consent is checked per channel on every path that selects recipients.
  - Istanbul, istanbul and ISTANBUL all match İstanbul; an unknown city is an
    error, never a silent 0 users.
  - Fixed dates use the campaign's time zone and never shift.
  - AI answers are checked strictly. Nothing is saved on failure, and the raw AI
    text never reaches the browser.
  - Draft edits are protected against two marketers editing at once.
  - The new build caches are safe: nothing lands in the image, and make test
    never touches your data.
  - No secrets in the branch.
  - The reviewer agreed with all of my T4–T6 choices.

  Critical: the mock AI can drop or widen audience conditions
  - Where: internal/llm/mock.go:68. The free-text "offer" part of a brief (for
    example "30% discount reminder") accepts up to 100 of any characters. The
    guard against audience words in it only knows a few words ("who", "users",
    "kullanıcı"…).
  - Confirmed: I re-checked two of the reviewer's five examples myself:
    - "Send a discount to everyone in Izmir and to users in Istanbul." → only
      İstanbul users; Izmir is silently dropped.
    - "Ankara'daki tüm kullanıcılara sadece kadınlar için indirim gönder." → all
      Ankara users; "women only" is dropped.
    - The reviewer's other three examples drop a 30-day no-order condition, a
      "women only" restriction, and a "not VIPs" exclusion.
  - Why it matters: this breaks D15's core rule that the AI never drops or
    broadens a condition. In my T5 report I said the mock "never guesses"; that
    wasn't fully true.
  - Fix: limit the offer to a short phrase (at most 4 words), reject words like
    "for", "only", "except", "and", "için", "sadece", "hariç", "ve", and add all
    five briefs as tests. All 16 documented example briefs still fit.

  Important: real mode retries failed AI calls, against D15
  - Where: cmd/scc/main.go:83. The Anthropic Go SDK retries failed calls twice
    by default; I confirmed this in the SDK source. My test turned the retries
    off, so it didn't catch this.
  - Effect: a server error can trigger up to 3 calls per attempt. With our one
    validation retry, that's up to 6 paid calls for one brief, and the error
    reaches the marketer later.
  - Fix: one line, turning off the SDK's own retries inside NewAnthropic, plus a
    test without that override.

  Minor
  1. Wrong time-zone offset from Claude. If Claude returns "10:00" with the
     wrong offset (UTC instead of +03:00), the proposed time silently shifts by
     3 hours. The marketer would still see it before approving. Fix: treat it as
     invalid and let the existing retry correct it.
  2. Same field twice. Rules may not repeat a field and operator, but the prompt
     doesn't tell Claude. "Bought books in 30 days and electronics in 90 days"
     would end in an "invalid answer" error (502) instead of "unsupported". Fix:
     one sentence in the prompt.
  3. "In 0 hours". The mock accepts "Send it in 0 hours" as a send time of right
     now. Approval (T9) would reject it anyway. Fix: start hours at 1.
  4. Unused channels field. Claude is still asked for channels that we then
     ignore. It costs tokens and can cause pointless retries. Fix (optional):
     remove the field from the schema and the prompt.

  Not judged by the reviewer:
  - Real Claude calls, which need an API key.
  - Whether 60 seconds is long enough for Claude at high effort.
  - A brief containing </brief> can close the data boundary early. The brief
    only comes from the logged-in marketer and the answer is validated anyway,
    so the reviewer didn't count it.

  My recommendation: fix the critical, the important and minor items 1–3 before
  merging, each with a failing test first and the full suite after; they're all
  small. Item 4 removes code and fits D1, which says the marketer chooses the
  channels, so I'd include it too, but it's optional.

  Should I fix them on milestone-b-audience, push, and come back to you for the
  merge decision?

⏺ T7 is done and committed locally on the new branch milestone-c-content,
  created from main (1c8bc78). Nothing is pushed yet, and I haven't started T8.

  What T7 adds (internal/content)
  - Variables: only the exact tokens {{first_name}}, {{last_name}} and {{city}}
    are allowed. Any other use of { or } is an error, including {{ first_name 
    }}, {{FIRST_NAME}}, {{email}}, {first_name}, unclosed {{first_name and
    literal text like {SALE}.
  - Personalizing: values are filled in a fixed order, runs of spaces collapse
    to one, and spaces at the start and end are trimmed. Line breaks stay (D13).
    Lengths count Turkish letters as one character (D2).
  - Checks before approval:
    - Content must exist for exactly the chosen channels.
    - SMS has no subject; the email subject is a single line.
    - Text that's over the limit even with every variable empty is an error
      (D3). A 172-character SMS is rejected; 150 characters plus {{first_name}}
      is accepted.
    - Templates are also capped at twice the limit, so unchecked AI output can't
      send huge templates into the database estimate.
  - Over-limit estimate: one database query per channel counts how many
    recipients would get a message over the limit. It personalizes in SQL
    exactly like the Go code, without building every message in memory (D3).

  Results

  Two things worth knowing
  1. A gap the mutation check exposed. My first SQL-vs-Go test never produced a
     double space, because none of the templates had an empty variable between
     two spaces. So it couldn't detect SQL forgetting to tidy spaces. A new test
     (TestEstimate_TidiesLikeRender) uses a user without a last name and
     templates where tidying decides "over or not". Both tidying mutations are
     now caught.
  2. My test asked for more than the spec. I expected "Dear Ali , hi" to become
     "Dear Ali, hi". The plan only says "collapse spaces and trim", and D12
     explicitly accepts awkward text when a value is empty, so I changed the
     test to match the spec. This affects only users without a last name, about
     3% of the seed data. Option: I could also remove a space before punctuation
     (, . ! ?) and at line ends. It's small, but the SQL estimate would need
     the same rule and test.

  Commits (local only)
  - 1af8aa2 feat: add message template validation, rendering and over-limit
    estimate
  - a2c60e1 docs: describe the content package in the README

  Choices I made
  - Braces are reserved for the three variables, so literal braces can't be used
    in messages.
  - The estimate takes one "audience" (a filter plus its values) per channel, so
    it works the same for normal campaigns and for later resends to a fixed
    list of recipients.

  Decisions for you
  1. Push: should I push milestone-c-content?
  2. Punctuation tidying: add the extra tidying described above, or keep the
     spec as it is?
  3. T8 (streamed content generation) waits for your approval.

⏺ T8 (streamed content generation) is done and committed locally on
  milestone-c-content; the four commits aren't pushed. As you decided, the
  tidying stays as specified, and the branch with T7 was pushed before I
  started.

  What T8 adds

  Part: llm.FieldStreamer (D34)
  What it does: Turns the AI's JSON, arriving in arbitrary pieces, into live
  text
  per field (sms.body, email.subject, email.body). It handles escapes, \n and
  Turkish letters cut mid-character.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Part: Mock content
  What it does: Fixed, valid messages in the brief's language (Turkish or
  English) with {{first_name}}, for the chosen channels only, streamed in small
   chunks.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Part: Claude content
  What it does: Streaming call with the channels' schema. The prompt states the
  allowed variables, the limits (160 / 80 / 2,000), plain-text email, "don't
  invent facts", and the brief's language. A refusal or a cut-off answer
  becomes an error.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Part: POST /api/campaigns/{id}/content
  What it does: Event stream: delta while writing, retry if the first answer is
  invalid, then done (with the saved content and new version) or error. Saved
  only if valid, the draft is unchanged, and the browser is still connected.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Part: PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/content
  What it does: Saves messages edited by hand, with the same checks. A single
  campaign from GET now includes its content.

  Results

  Check: make test
  Result: all 9 packages pass (45 s)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Streamer, split at every byte position plus byte by byte
  Result: output identical to Go's own JSON parser; no broken characters
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: API cases
  Result: streams deltas then done; invalid → retry → done; invalid twice →
  error
  with nothing saved; AI refusal or outage → error with no internal details and
   no retry; browser disconnect → nothing saved; stale version, unknown id or
  non-draft → normal 404/409/422 before any streaming
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Mutation: holding back partly received characters disabled
  Result: caught (a split İ leaked)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Mutation: disconnect guards
  Result: see below
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: make lint, go mod tidy, build
  Result: clean; each intermediate commit also builds on its own
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Live with the mock
  Result: 24 events over about 0.75 s, Turkish content, saved at version 2, 0
  messages

  Worth knowing
  - The disconnect test has two guards behind it. Removing only my explicit
    "browser left" check didn't make the test fail, because the save also uses
    the request's context, and that is cancelled when the browser leaves.
    Removing both made it fail. So the protection is double, and the test
    catches it when both are gone.
  - An accidental live confirmation: my first live check used a broken viewer
    script that closed the stream early. The server treated that as a disconnect
    and correctly saved nothing.
  - Real Claude: content generation is tested only against the fake local API
    server, not against the real API.

  Choices I made
  - Content generation sends the draft version; if someone edited the draft
    meanwhile, the result isn't saved.
  - The mock writes generic messages, not specific to the offer (e.g. "%30
    indirim" isn't mentioned). It's enough for the demo; real mode writes to the
    brief.
  - Claude writes content at medium effort, so text starts appearing sooner;
    reading the audience stays at high effort.
  - The mock pauses 30 ms between chunks so streaming is visible in the UI.

  - f94f7a2 feat: generate message content with the mock or streaming Claude
  - 4aac81e feat: add streamed content generation and manual content edits API
  - 6fbdaa4 docs: document content generation endpoints

  database. Drafts 1–3 are untouched.

      207 +func FixedWindowStarts(rules []Rule, loc *time.Location) ([]ti
          +me.Time, error) {                                             
      208 +        var starts []time.Time                                
      209 +        for i, r := range rules {                             
      210 +                c, err := parse(r, nil)

      212 +                        return nil, fmt.Errorf("rule %d: %w",

      229 +        }

      58  Messages may use only `{{first_name}}`, `{{last_name}}` and `{{

⏺ T9 (approve, cancel, estimate, resend) is done and committed locally on
  milestone-c-content; the three commits aren't pushed. That completes Milestone
  C (T7–T9). As you asked, the branch with T8 was pushed before I started.

  What T9 adds

  Endpoint: POST …/approve
  Behaviour: The send choice must be explicit: "now" or {"at": …} between 1
  minute and 30 days ahead; a time suggested from the brief never applies by
  itself. It checks the version, that the campaign is a draft, valid content, a
   non-empty audience, and that no fixed-date window starts after the send
  time. It stores the current per-channel counts and over-limit numbers as
  approval_estimate. No messages are  created (D30).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Endpoint: POST …/cancel
  Behaviour: Works only on approved or sending campaigns. Only messages that are

  still waiting are cancelled; messages in flight or already final are left
  alone (D11).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Endpoint: GET …/estimate
  Behaviour: Current audience and over-limit counts, labelled "estimate": true.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Endpoint: POST …/resend
  Behaviour: After a campaign completes or is cancelled: a draft for the
  recipients skipped for length on that channel. Its audience is fixed, and its
   rules can't be edited. One active resend per campaign and channel.

  Choices I made
  1. "Minus anyone sent" (D31): inside one campaign a skipped user can't also
     have been sent to, so I applied it to earlier resends that were cancelled
     midway. A new resend excludes anyone already reached on that channel by the
     parent or by any earlier resend of it.
  2. Fixed-date window after the send time: rejected for every rule type.
     "Matches nobody", as in the plan, is true for most rules, but for "no order
     since 1 November" it would match everyone.
  3. Cancel needs no version: a safety stop shouldn't fail because someone's
     screen is out of date. Cancelling a draft gets 409, since D11 only allows
     approved or sending campaigns.
  4. approval_estimate: it also stores the over-limit counts and when they were
     calculated, so the dashboard (T14) can show the estimate next to the real
     numbers.

  Commits (local only)
  - 71d86ba feat: expose fixed window starts and consent column from audience
  - a7f2706 feat: add approve (now or scheduled), cancel, estimate and resend
  - 42a2fb4 docs: document approval, cancel, estimate and resend endpoints

  Decisions for you
  1. Push, then a PR for Milestone C with an independent review, as for
     Milestones A and B?
  2. Before Milestone D (sending): the plan marks these defaults as "confirm
     before T10/T12", and they're still under Open Questions:
     - mock provider: 20–200 ms delay, 5% server errors, 3% "too many requests"
       (429) with a 1-second wait, 1% timeouts;
     - lease 30 s, provider timeout 5 s, 20 workers per channel.

     I'll ask you about them before starting T10.

  and C.

      UTC.

      exactly the right recipients.

      secrets and README accuracy, and unnecessary complexity.

✻ Brewed for 52s · done 5:22 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Milestone C review (T7–T9), PR #3, main..milestone-c-content (42a2fb4)

  I reviewed every changed Go file in one pass. I ran `make test`: all packages
  passed (campaign 33s, content 10s, llm, db, audience, auth). I did not run
  `make lint`. I ran a probe test (FieldStreamer, Render, ValidateDraft) in a
  copy of the repo at /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-[user]-smart-campaign-compose
  r/70eb9231-9c35-41a8-8ccd-36cb1ed9bd35/scratchpad/repo/probe/probe_test.go. I
  ran one read-only SELECT on the app's Postgres. No repo files, demo data or PR
  were touched.

  ### Strengths
  - **Write paths are correct.** Approve locks the row with `FOR UPDATE` and
  re-checks status and version. Content, rules and schedule writes all go
  through `UPDATE … WHERE version=$2 AND status='draft'`. Cancel takes the same
  row lock. Approve reads content through the pool rather than the transaction,
  but that is safe: every content write first updates the locked campaign row,
  so it waits for approve and then fails on the version check. I found no race
  that changes content or rules after approval, and no approve/cancel
  interleaving that leaves an inconsistent state.
  - **SQL is safe.** `renderSQL` only puts constant variable names into the SQL
  text. Template text goes in as `$n::text`. The resend WHERE clause and
  approve/cancel use only `$n` arguments plus the constant consent column.
  - **Go and SQL rendering match:** same replace order, same `' {2,}'` collapse,
  the same trim of ASCII spaces only, `coalesce` on the nullable `last_name`
  (`first_name` and `city` are NOT NULL), and `char_length` counts the same
  characters as Go's rune count.
  - **The streaming handler is solid.** Version is checked before headers are
  sent. There is a timeout per attempt, there is no server WriteTimeout, and no
  middleware wraps the writer, so flushing works. Content is saved only when
  valid, the version is unchanged and the client is still connected. "error"
  events carry fixed messages, never raw AI text. The FieldStreamer did not
  panic on stray `}}]]` input.
  - **Resend:** the partial unique index makes double clicks and two marketers
  safe. Consent applies, rules cannot be changed (PUT rules returns 409) and the
  audience is never recalculated from rules. `parseSend` enforces the now + 1
  minute window and keeps the offset, so a non-UTC time is stored as the
  intended instant.

  ### Issues

  **Critical:** none.

  **Important:** none.

  **Minor**

  1. **A template made only of variables passes validation and can render an
  empty message.**
     - Where: `internal/content/template.go:139-155`.
     - Scenario: SMS body `{{last_name}}`, or an email subject `{{last_name}}`.
  Probe: `ValidateDraft` returns `[]` and `Render` returns `""` for a user
  without a last name. Every recipient with a NULL `last_name` would get an
  empty SMS or subject. D3 does not skip empty messages at send time.
     - Fix (one line in `checkTemplate`): reject when
  `strings.TrimSpace(Render(tpl, UserVars{})) == ""` ("needs some fixed text").

  2. **A NUL character gives a 500 instead of a 422, and the AI path does not
  retry.**
     - Where: `template.go:139` (no check), `content.go:157-163` and
  `content.go:92-94`.
     - Scenario: `PUT /content` with `"body":"Hi\u0000"` passes `ValidateDraft`
  (probe confirmed). The insert then fails, because Postgres rejects 0x00
  (confirmed: `invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0x00`), and the user
  gets a 500 "internal error". If Claude emits `\u0000`, the stream ends with
  "internal error" instead of the D15 retry.
     - Fix: in `checkTemplate`, reject `strings.ContainsRune(tpl, 0)`. Rejecting
  all control characters except `\n` (and `\t`) would be better.

  3. **A user value containing another variable token is expanded.**
     - Where: `template.go:80-86` and `estimate.go:71-81`.
     - Scenario: `first_name = "{{city}}"`. `Render("Hi {{first_name}} from
  {{city}}")` gives `"Hi Ankara from Ankara"` (probe). Go and SQL behave the
  same, so the estimate is still correct. The effect is only on recipients whose
  data contains a literal token.
     - Fix: `strings.NewReplacer` in Go (single pass). SQL would then need a
  matching single-pass form, so accepting and documenting this is reasonable.

  4. **The FieldStreamer's live preview can differ from what is saved.**
     - Where: `internal/llm/fieldstream.go:108`.
     - Scenario: `{"SMS":{"Body":"hello"}}` is accepted by `encoding/json`,
  which matches keys case-insensitively and so passes `DisallowUnknownFields`;
  the stream shows nothing. With `{"sms":{"body":"a","body":"b"}}`, json keeps
  `"b"` while the stream shows `"ab"` (both confirmed by probe).
     - Impact: display only; the `done` event carries the right content, and the
  structured-output schema makes this unlikely with Claude. Fix if wanted: have
  the UI replace the fields from `done`, or reject duplicate or non-exact keys
  in `parseContent`.

  5. **A max_tokens cutoff is reported as "The AI service is unavailable".**
     - Where: `anthropic_content.go:53-54` with `content.go:129-131`.
     - Effect: the message is misleading, the event is logged at Error level,
  and there is no retry.
     - Fix: a sentinel error with its own message, or treat it like invalid
  output and use the retry.

  6. **A resend is generated from the parent's brief with no "shorter"
  instruction.**
     - Where: `content.go:62`.
     - Scenario: a resend draft's POST /content sends the same brief and the
  same "keep under 130" prompt as the original. Claude is likely to write copy
  of similar length, so the same long-name users are skipped again. The plan
  says "writes a shorter template".
     - Fix: if `parent_campaign_id` is set, add a line asking for a shorter text
  (e.g. an SMS of at most ~100 characters). This is a prompt and scope
  decision, so it needs the engineer's approval.

  7. **The README describes content-generation failures wrongly.**
     - Where: README API section, the sentence that ends "…returns 502/504 and
  nothing is saved".
     - Problem: this sentence now sits under the content endpoints. Generation
  failures there are an `error` SSE event inside a 200 response, not 502/504.
  Say that the 502/504 behaviour applies to `POST /api/campaigns`.

  ### Declined to judge
  - A cancelled resend's messages still in `sending` (attempts already in
  progress) could become `sent` after a later resend's audience is built. That
  depends on the Milestone D worker.
  - `srv.Shutdown` does not cancel request contexts, so a generation running
  during shutdown could still save. This is shutdown behaviour outside T8.
  - A brief containing `</brief>` could break the prompt framing. This is the
  same pattern as T5, and the brief is capped at 1,000 characters (D15).
  - There is no limit on parallel generations per campaign, which costs API
  credits. No requirement covers it.
  - The schedule check was confirmed only by reading the code; the tests cover
  the 1 minute and 30 day limits.

  ### Rulings
  All twelve rulings are sound.
  - **T7.** The space-only tidy is the agreed D12/D13 behaviour. "Any other
  brace is an error" matches the plan. The 2× raw cap is fine. Passing
  `map[channel]Audience` is the simpler, correct shape.
  - **T8.** The raw JSON / FieldStreamer split, the required version, the fixed
  mock templates and medium effort are all fine.
  - **T9.** The "sent by the parent or any earlier resend" rule is correct and
  actually needed: cancelling a resend mid-send allows a new resend under the
  index, and without the rule those users would get the message twice. Rejecting
  a fixed window that starts after the send time, cancel without a version, and
  storing over-limit counts plus `calculated_at` are all fine.

  ### Verdict
  **Ready to merge: Yes, with optional small fixes.** I found no correctness,
  concurrency or SQL-safety problem. I recommend fixing #1 (empty message) and
  #2 (NUL) before merge (about two lines in `checkTemplate` plus tests) and the
  #7 README sentence. #6 needs the engineer's decision. The rest can wait.

  Not checked: lint was not run, and there was no live end-to-end test against
  the real Claude API (mock and unit tests only).

⏺ I re-checked findings 1, 2 and 7 myself against the code before reporting:
  checkTemplate has no "is there any fixed text?" check and no control-character

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Agent "Review Milestone C branch" finished · 5m 13s

⏺ The independent review of PR #3 found no critical or important problems, but
  I'd fix a few small things before merging. It found 7 minor issues and judged
  all 12 of my T7–T9 choices sound. I haven't changed anything; PR #3 is still
  open, not merged, and mergeable.

  What held up
  - Concurrency: two approvals, or an approval racing a cancel or an edit, can't
    leave a campaign half-changed. Every write checks the version and status,
    and approve and cancel lock the campaign row. No path lets content or rules
    change after approval.
  - SQL: template text reaches Postgres only as query arguments; only fixed
    variable names are written into the SQL. The database estimate renders
    exactly like the Go code.
  - Content stream: the version is checked before streaming starts, each attempt
    has its own timeout, and content is saved only when it's valid, the draft
    is unchanged and the browser is still connected. Error events never contain
    raw AI text.
  - Resend: two clicks or two marketers can't create two follow-ups; consent
    applies and the audience can't be changed.

  Minor findings (number them back to me to have them fixed)

  1. A message made only of variables can be sent empty. content/template.go:139
     - An SMS body or email subject of just {{last_name}} passes the checks, and
       the reviewer confirmed it renders as an empty text for recipients
       without a last name. Nothing at send time catches that.
     - I rate this higher than the reviewer did, because real people would
       receive an empty SMS.
     - Fix: require some fixed text. One line plus a test.
  2. A NUL character causes a 500 error. template.go:139
     - A message containing \u0000 passes the checks, then the database refuses
       it, and the marketer gets "internal error" instead of a clear 422.
     - If Claude ever produces one, the generation fails instead of using the
       one retry D15 allows.
     - Fix: reject control characters except line breaks and tabs.
  3. A user value containing a variable gets expanded.
     - A first name stored literally as {{city}} turns "Hi {{first_name}} from
       {{city}}" into "Hi Ankara from Ankara". The estimate stays correct, since
       Go and SQL behave the same.
     - Only matters for names containing literal variable text. I'd accept it
       and document it, because a fix would complicate the SQL estimate.
  4. The live preview can differ from what's saved.
     - The reviewer confirmed this for keys written in different case, or
       repeated. The final done event always has the right content, and Claude's
       forced JSON format makes it unlikely.
     - Fix: when the UI is built (T17), it should show the content from done.
       I'll note it for T17.
  5. A too-long Claude answer is reported as "AI service unavailable". It's
     misleading, logged as an error, and not retried. Fix: give it its own
     message and treat it like an invalid answer, with one retry.
  6. Resend content isn't asked to be shorter. content.go:62
     - Generating content for a resend uses the same brief and the same "under
       130 characters" instruction as the original. The likely result is a text
       of similar length, and the same long-name recipients get skipped again.
     - The plan says the marketer writes a shorter template for a resend. This
       is a prompt and scope change, so it's your call: should I add an
       instruction like "SMS of at most ~100 characters" when generating for a
       resend, and make the mock shorter for resends too?
  7. A wrong README sentence. It says failures return 502/504, but it now sits
     under the content endpoints, where a failure is an error event inside the
     stream. Fix: say it applies to POST /api/campaigns only.

  Not judged by the reviewer:
  - Messages of a cancelled resend that were already in flight; that depends on
    the Milestone D worker.
  - A generation running during server shutdown.
  - A brief containing </brief>, the same pattern as in T5.
  - Unlimited parallel generations per campaign, which only cost API credits.

  My recommendation:
  - Fix before merging: 1, 2, 5 and 7. All small, each with a failing test
    first.
  - Accept and document: 3, in the code and README.
  - Note for T17: 4.
  - Decide: 6. My suggestion is yes: add the "shorter" instruction for resends.

  Should I go ahead like that? And should I include 6?

⏺ The package doesn't exist yet, so I'll build it in three parts: the migration
  for the provider schema, idempotency table, and scoped scc_provider role;
  db.SetProviderPassword; and the provider server. Since roles are shared across
  the Postgres server and migrations run in parallel, role creation needs to
  handle concurrent attempts safely.

⏺ I found the root cause: all three failures stem from jsonb reformatting the
  stored response, making the original and replayed answers differ byte-for-byte
  despite sharing the same ID. I'll fix this by sending the first answer via
  RETURNING so it matches exactly what's stored for future replays.

⏺ T10 (the mock SMS and email provider) is done and committed locally on the new
  branch milestone-d-sending, created from main. Nothing is pushed yet, and I
  haven't started T11.

  Approved defaults recorded (51ed22e): the mock provider's behaviour is in D20,
  and the worker values (lease 30 s, provider timeout 5 s, 20 requests per
  channel) are in D19, each with a dated note. Open Questions is now empty.

  What T10 adds
  - A mock-provider service in Compose (CASE §8). It serves POST /sms/send and
    POST /email/send, and every request needs an Idempotency-Key, which will be
    the message ID.
  - Simulated behaviour per channel, adjustable via .env:
    - the approved defaults: 20–200 ms delay, 5% server errors, 3% "too many
      requests" (429) with Retry-After: 1, 1% hangs past the client's timeout;
    - a fixed seed (PROVIDER_SEED), so test runs repeat.
  - Idempotency (D6):
    - The key is stored before anything is simulated, and a repeated key gets
      the stored answer, byte for byte.
    - A key still being processed gets a retryable 503 with Retry-After.
    - Errors and 429s release the key, so a retry is actually processed.
    - A "timeout" is a lost response: the message is accepted after the hang,
      and the retry receives the stored answer instead of a second send.
    - A key left stuck by a crash can be taken over after a minute.
  - Separate database role (D6): the provider logs in as scc_provider, which can
    only use its own provider schema; a test proves it can't read users.
    migrate sets the role's password from PROVIDER_DB_PASSWORD, and Compose has
    a default.

⏺ T11 (the sending queue) is done and committed locally on milestone-d-sending;
  the three commits aren't pushed. As you asked, the branch with T10 was pushed
  first. I haven't started T12.

  What T11 adds (internal/worker)

  Function: BuildAudience
  What it does: Starts every approved campaign whose send time has come, one
  transaction per campaign. It works out the audience at that  moment (D30), or
   uses a resend's fixed list, and creates one message per recipient and
  channel. The campaign becomes sending, or completed if nobody matches.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Function: Claim
  What it does: Hands a worker up to n due messages of one channel: pending,
  retries whose time has come, or messages whose 30-second lease ran out
  because a worker crashed. Every claim counts as an attempt.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Function: Complete
  What it does: Records the provider attempt (outcome, HTTP status, timings) and

  applies the result only if this worker still holds the lease. A retry for a
  campaign cancelled meanwhile becomes cancelled.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Function: FinishCampaigns
  What it does: Marks a sending campaign completed once every message is final.

  Results

  Check: make test
  Result: all 11 packages pass (74 s); 14 new queue tests
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Plan cases
  Result: built only at the send time, not a second earlier; someone who orders
  before the send is dropped and a new match is added; a cancelled campaign is
  never built; a crash midway leaves nothing and the rerun builds cleanly; an
  empty audience completes; resends use exactly the skipped list
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Mutation: Claim without row locking
  Result: caught: the same message was claimed up to 3 times
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Mutation: Complete without the lease check
  Result: caught: a stale worker overwrote the result
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Mutation: BuildAudience without row locking
  Result: caught: campaigns were built several times over
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: make lint, build
  Result: clean, after one formatting fix that lint caught

  Worth knowing
  - One test of mine was wrong. By its second claim, a minute later, the first
    message's 30-second lease had expired, and the queue correctly took it back.
    I fixed the test to complete that message first, as a real worker would.
  - A known race for T12 to cover. If a campaign is cancelled at the very moment
    a retry is being scheduled, that retry can stay "waiting" on the cancelled
    campaign. T12 checks the campaign's status before every send, so it can
    never actually be sent. I've noted it in the progress log.
  - Preparation commit: the resend audience filter moved into the audience
    package, so the API and the worker share one implementation. Behaviour is
    unchanged and the existing tests guard it.

  - 23f069e feat: add sending queue: audience build, claim with lease, results
  - c83074c docs: describe the sending queue

  unchanged.

⏺ I've confirmed the overshoot originates inside the worker itself: the rate
  limiter spaces out reservations evenly, but goroutine wake-up delays under
  load cause actual departures to cluster, pushing past the 100/sec cap. This
  invalidates D19's assumption that the limiter alone would enforce the limit,
  so I'll flag that decision for your call while I go fix the unrelated
  email-test data in the meantime.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · The rate-limit test failed: up to 110 requests per channel in some 
     1-second windows (limit 100), measured on the worker's own send times too. 
     Cause: x/time/rate spaces token *reservations* 10 ms apart, but goroutines 
     wake late under load, so departures bunch. D19 assumed the limiter alone 
     guarantees ≤100. How should I fix it? → Add a hard 1 s window cap 
     (Recommended)

⏺ I'll keep x/time/rate for smooth spacing while adding a hard cap on actual
  departures, starting with a unit test that spins up many goroutines in a small
  window and verifies departure times never exceed n per window.

      184  - **Why:** The simplest way to enforce the limit exactly and t

      185  - **Rejected:** *Shared token bucket in Postgres:* a database

       90  
       91  The key is stored before anything is simulated. A repeated key
            gets the stored result for a finished request, or a retryable
            503 with `Retry-After` while it is still in progress; a 5xx o
           r 429 releases the key so a retry is processed. Keys live in t
           he `provider` schema, which the provider reaches through its o
           wn database role (`scc_provider`, password `PROVIDER_DB_PASSWO
           RD`) that cannot read the app's tables (D6).

       93 +## Sending                                                    
       94 +                                                              
       95 +The `worker` service starts approved campaigns when their send
          + time comes, evaluating the audience at that moment (D30), and
          + sends each message through the provider with its message ID a
          +s `Idempotency-Key`. Before every request it re-checks that th
          +e campaign is still sending and the recipient still allows the
          + channel, and it personalizes and length-checks the text: over
          +-limit messages are skipped (`character_limit_exceeded`) and c
          +an be resent later (D31). 200 → `sent`; 429, 5xx, timeouts and
          + network errors are retried with exponential backoff and jitte
          +r (honouring `Retry-After`) up to `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5); other 4x
          +x → `failed`. Every attempt is recorded in `delivery_attempts`
          +.                                                             
       96 +                                                              
       97 +Per channel at most 100 provider requests leave in any second,
          + retries included: a rate limiter spaces them 10 ms apart and 
          +a hard cap counts actual departures (D19). The cap is exact on
          + the worker's side; at the provider, a request delayed by more
          + than 50 ms between leaving the queue and arriving could in th
          +eory still bunch with later ones.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ T12 (the sending worker) is done and committed locally on milestone-d-sending;
  the three commits aren't pushed. The system now actually sends campaigns
  through the mock provider. As you asked, the branch with T11 was pushed first,
  and I haven't started T13.

  What T12 adds
  - A worker service in Compose. Per channel, 20 senders each take one message
    at a time and go through these steps:
    a. Check that the campaign is still sending and the recipient still allows
       the channel; otherwise the message is cancelled or skipped.
    b. Personalize and length-check the text; over-limit messages are skipped
       (character_limit_exceeded). Skips use no rate-limit slot and make no
       provider call.
    c. Wait for the rate limit, then check again: a cancel arriving during the
       wait still stops the send (the plan's Review Focus #3).
    d. Send with the message ID as the Idempotency-Key.
  - Retry rules (D7): 200 means sent. 429, 5xx, timeouts and network errors are
    retried, waiting 1 s doubling up to 30 s with random jitter, and never
    sooner than the provider's Retry-After. Other 4xx means failed. After 5
    attempts (MAX_ATTEMPTS) the message fails with its last error.

  An unexpected problem, fixed as you decided. D19 assumed the rate limiter
  alone keeps each channel at 100 per second. The test proved otherwise: up to
  110 per second, even on the worker's own clock. The limiter spaces its
  reservations correctly, but senders wake up late under load and then leave in
  bunches.
  - The fix you chose: the limiter stays, for even spacing, plus a hard cap on
    actual departures (at most 100 per window of just over one second, about 30
    lines, no new dependency). D19 has a dated note.
  - A smaller second cause: one run still showed 102 at the provider. The HTTP
    client was opening a new connection for most requests; it now reuses
    connections.
  - Result: at most 100 per second on both sides, in 5 out of 5 runs, with
    retries included. It's guaranteed on the worker side; at the provider it
    could only be exceeded if a request stalled for more than 50 ms on its way
    out. The README says so.

  Results

  Check: make test
  Result: all 11 packages pass (69 s), including the rate test under full load
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Retry-rule cases
  Result: 200, 429 with Retry-After, 500, 503, timeout, unreachable provider,
  400, and the 5th attempt failing
  ────────────────────────────────────────

  Three problems found and fixed along the way:
  1. A failing test hung instead of failing. My helpers checked results while a
     database query was still open, so a failed check leaked the connection and
     cleanup waited forever (until Go's 10-minute limit). Now they read all rows
     first, and the same bug fails cleanly in 7.5 s.
  2. A gap in the resend tests. In every fixture, the parent's recipients were
     either skipped or sent, so a resend to "everyone not yet sent" looked
     identical to the correct one. The fixtures now include a recipient whose
     message failed and one skipped for missing consent, and that bug is caught
     in both the API and worker tests.

✻ Churned for 1m 17s · done 6:53 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Milestone D review (T10–T13), PR #4: verdict "with fixes"

  I reviewed the whole diff main..3e2acc9 against the plan, CASE.md and
  DECISIONS.md. I ran `make test` once and all 12 packages passed (worker 36 s,
  e2e 44 s). I did not run `make lint`. I made one probe, a throwaway Go program
  in a container, to confirm finding 1.

  ### What is done well
  - The idempotency design is sound. The key is the message ID, the same on
  every attempt. The provider stores the key before it simulates a send. A 5xx
  or 429 releases the key, and a timeout becomes a lost response that is
  replayed on the next attempt. I traced the hang path with the default settings
  (attempts at 0, ≥5.5, ≥6.5, ≥8.5 and ≥12.5 s, against the provider accepting
  at 10 s): the last attempt always lands after the acceptance and gets the
  replay.
  - Claim, Complete and BuildAudience lock rows correctly. SKIP LOCKED plus the
  recheck under lock means two claimers never share a row. Complete is fenced by
  the lease token. Cancel and BuildAudience serialise on `FOR UPDATE`.
  - The windowCap logic is correct. A new departure is allowed only when the
  oldest of the last n is at least one window old. It uses the monotonic clock
  and records the departure before the request.
  - The e2e helper counts real sends per key (200 responses that are not
  replays), which is the right thing to measure. The mutation testing found real
  gaps.

  ### Important

  **1. A worker shutdown during the last attempt marks a delivered message
  `failed`.**
  - Where: `internal/worker/dispatch.go:228` with `:303-311`, which contradicts
  the comment at `:118-119` ("A message interrupted by shutdown keeps its
  lease").
  - Scenario: SIGTERM arrives while attempt 5 is in flight. `Client.Do` returns
  `context canceled`; the probe confirmed it is neither a deadline nor a
  timeout, so it is classified `network_error`. `done()` then writes the result
  through `WithoutCancel`, and attempt 5 of 5 becomes `failed`. Meanwhile the
  provider ignores the hang-up (`provider.go:114`) and accepts the message.
  Result: failed although accepted. On attempts 1–4 the shutdown also burns an
  attempt that should not count.
  - Smallest fix: send the HTTP request with `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)`. The
  client's 5 s timeout still bounds it, and Docker's 10 s grace period covers
  it. Alternatively, return `ctx.Err()` without calling Complete, so the lease
  expires and the next attempt gets the replay.

  **2. A cancelled resend followed by a new resend can send the same SMS or
  email twice (Review Focus #9, D31 against D11).**
  - Where: `internal/audience/sql.go` `BuildResend`. It only excludes users with
  `s.status = 'sent'`.
  - Scenario:
    1. Resend R1 is sending. One message hits the provider's hang path: the
  worker times out and schedules a retry, and the provider accepts it 10 s
  later.
    2. The marketer cancels R1. Per D11 the `retry_scheduled` message becomes
  `cancelled`, although it was delivered.
    3. Because R1 is cancelled, the one-active-resend index (`status <>
  'cancelled'`) lets the marketer create R2.
    4. R2 is built. That user is not `sent` anywhere, so R2 includes them under
  a new message ID, which is a new key, and they get the channel twice.
  - Messages still `sending` when R2 is built with "send now" fall into the same
  gap.
  - Smallest fix: in `BuildResend`, also exclude users whose message on that
  channel is `sending`, or had an attempt with an unknown result (`timeout` or
  `network_error`). This needs the engineer's decision first, because those
  users might then never get the message.

  ### Minor

  **3. The window cap is waited for after the cancel recheck.**
  - Where: `dispatch.go:216-228`.
  - The limiter allows about 105 requests per 1.05 s while the cap allows 100,
  so at full load the cap is the binding limit and goroutines regularly wait in
  `cap.wait` for tens of milliseconds.
  - Scenario: a cancel that commits during that wait is followed by a provider
  call, which goes against Review Focus #3 as written. Also, the real throughput
  is about 95/s, not 100.
  - Fix: call `caps[ch].wait` before the second `load` and `skip`. The cost is a
  wasted slot when a message turns out to be cancelled.

  **4. Shutdown or a lost lease after the provider accepted can leave a
  delivered message `cancelled` or `skipped`.**
  - Where: `dispatch.go:204, 220`.
  - Scenario: the worker crashes after the provider accepted a message but
  before Complete. The campaign is then cancelled, or the user opts out. When
  the message is claimed again, the skip check runs before any provider call, so
  the replay never happens. The message is recorded `cancelled` or `no_consent`
  although it was delivered.
  - Mainly a metrics error, but it feeds into finding 2. Same remedy as 2, or
  skip the skip check when the message already has an attempt with an unknown
  result.

  **5. Mock provider delays have no upper bound, which can break the stale-key
  takeover.**
  - Where: `config.go`, the `loadProvider` `ms()` validation.
  - Scenario: `SMS_DELAY_MS_MIN=SMS_DELAY_MS_MAX=65000` with `MAX_ATTEMPTS=10`.
  Retries after 60 s take over the key while the original request is still
  asleep, both requests complete, and the provider "sends" twice under one key
  with two different IDs.
  - Fix: cap the delay below `StaleAfter`, for example at 30 s.

  **6. Special characters in `PROVIDER_DB_PASSWORD` break the mock provider.**
  - Where: `docker-compose.yml`, mock-provider `DATABASE_URL`.
  - Scenario: `PROVIDER_DB_PASSWORD='p@ss/word1'`. Migrate accepts it (it sets
  the password with `%L`), but the password goes unencoded into a URL, so
  mock-provider fails to start and the worker's health dependency never passes.
  - Fix: document the allowed characters in `.env.example`, or pass PGPASSWORD
  separately.

  **7. The e2e test can pass while delivery is wrong.**
  - Where: `e2e_test.go:195-212`.
  - `checkDelivery` checks "sent ⇒ provider sent it once" but not the converse.
  A worker that marked accepted messages `failed` (finding 1) or `cancelled`
  would pass, because only 90% need to be sent.
  - Fix: also require that every key the provider sent is `sent`, or explicitly
  list the allowed exceptions (last attempt timed out).

  **8. Timing assumptions can make tests flaky on a loaded machine.**
  - e2e case 2 (`:255-262`): approval has about 1 s of margin, and the "before
  send_at" check needs the test to wake within 500 ms.
  - `TestRun_RateLimitNoWindowOver100` provider-side check
  (`dispatch_test.go:318-329`): it relies on network jitter staying under 50 ms
  while the whole suite runs in parallel.
  - Neither failed in my run. Fix: widen the margins, or keep the provider-side
  check as a log only, since the worker-side check is the guarantee.

  ### Declined to judge
  - A worker restart within the same second exceeding 100/s: a restart takes
  more than 1 s, and D19 requires exactly one replica.
  - Planner behaviour of `UPDATE … WHERE id IN (subquery LIMIT … SKIP LOCKED)`
  re-running the subquery, and whether Claim stays fast on a large messages
  table (bitmap-or of the two partial indexes): not measured.
  - `Retry-After` given as an HTTP date is ignored (`dispatch.go:328`): the mock
  only sends seconds, and the backoff still applies.
  - A mock provider killed during a hang leaves the key in progress for 60 s, so
  those messages end `failed` (truthfully, they were not sent): accepted under
  D7.
  - The last attempt timing out while the provider accepts later ends `failed`:
  inherent to at-least-once delivery with capped attempts (D5, D7).
  - BuildAudience stopping at one failing campaign: I found no deterministic
  trigger, because time zones and rules are validated before approval.
  - Messages stuck after the content join fails: content is required and cannot
  change after approval.

  ### Rulings on the executor's deviations (Task 10–13)
  - **T10:** 503 + Retry-After for a key still in progress (fits D6), releasing
  the key on 5xx/429, the 1-minute stale-key takeover, the role created in
  migration 2 with its password set by migrate, and returning the stored jsonb
  answer: all agree. The stale takeover needs the delay bound from finding 5.
  - **T11:** the shared BuildResend, FinishCampaigns taking `now`, counting
  every claim as an attempt, and recording the attempt when the lease was lost:
  all agree. The retry-vs-cancel race really is closed by the recheck at send
  time; the only cost is one extra claim.
  - **T12:** the cap on top of the limiter is justified by the 106–110/s
  measured, and connection reuse is sensible. Disagree with the order of the
  recheck and the cap wait (finding 3). Note the effective rate is about 95/s.
  - **T13:** the test-only clock lag is acceptable, and production is unchanged.
  The fixes from mutation testing (fixtures, rows held open, shared provider
  password) are good. The delivery assertion is one-sided (finding 7).

  ### Is it ready to merge
  With fixes. Fix 1 is a small change. Finding 2 needs the engineer's decision
  on the resend rule before it is fixed. Findings 3–8 are small and can be fixed
  now or recorded. The core at-least-once, idempotency and rate-limit design
  holds.

  I did not run `make lint`, and I did not touch or check the live stack on
  :8080. The probe code is in /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-[user]-smart-campaign
  -composer/70eb9231-9c35-41a8-8ccd-36cb1ed9bd35/scratchpad/probe.

⏺ Before reporting, I checked the two important findings against the code
  myself. Both hold:
  - Finding 1: a cancelled request is neither a deadline nor a timeout in
    call(), so it's classified network_error.
  - Finding 2: BuildResend only excludes status = 'sent'.

  Recording the review in the log:

⏺ Agent "Review Milestone D branch" finished · 5m 28s

⏺ The independent review of PR #4 found 2 important issues, so it should not be
  merged as it stands. Both can wrongly report a delivered message, and one can
  send the same message twice. It also found 6 minor issues. I've checked both
  important issues against the code myself, and both are real. I haven't changed
  anything; PR #4 is open, not merged, and mergeable.

  What held up:
  - Idempotency design (D6): the reviewer traced the "lost response" case with
    the default settings, and the final retry always receives the stored answer.
  - Queue locking: no message is claimed twice, a stale worker can't overwrite a
    result, and cancel and audience-building take turns correctly.
  - The departure cap: its logic is correct.
  - The end-to-end test measures the right thing.
  - My T10–T13 choices were judged sound, except for the order described in
    minor issue 3.

  Important

  1. Stopping the worker can mark a delivered message failed
     (internal/worker/dispatch.go:228, :303)
     - If the worker shuts down during an attempt, the cancelled request is
       classified as a network error and uses up an attempt. On the 5th attempt,
       the message becomes failed.
     - But the provider ignores the hang-up and accepts the message, so "failed"
       is recorded although it was delivered. This also contradicts my own code
       comment ("a message interrupted by shutdown keeps its lease").
     - Fix: a shutdown must not count as an attempt. Return without a result and
       let the lease expire, so the next run gets the provider's stored answer.
  2. A cancelled resend followed by a new resend can send twice
     (internal/audience/sql.go, BuildResend)
     - Here's how it happens:
       i. In resend R1, a message's response is lost: the worker times out and
          schedules a retry, but the provider delivers it anyway.
       ii. The marketer cancels R1, so that message is recorded cancelled,
           although it was delivered.
       iii. Because R1 is cancelled, a new resend R2 is allowed.
       iv. R2 excludes only sent users, so this person gets the message a second
           time, under a new key.
     - The same happens to messages still in flight when R2 starts.
     - This needs your decision on the resend rule:
       - (A, my recommendation) Exclude anyone whose delivery is uncertain: a
         message still sending, or one with an attempt whose outcome is unknown
         (a timeout or network error). Nobody gets it twice, but a few people
         whose message really was lost won't get the resend either. That fits
         D31, "never resend to anyone else".
       - (B) Keep only excluding sent. Nobody is missed, but a duplicate is
         possible in this rare situation.

  Minor

  3. The cap wait comes after the last cancel check (dispatch.go:216). A cancel
     arriving during that short wait (tens of milliseconds at full load) can
     still be followed by a send. The real rate is also about 95 per second,
     not 100. Fix: wait for the cap first, then check.
  4. A delivered message can be recorded as cancelled or no_consent. If the
     worker crashes after delivery, and the campaign is then cancelled or the
     person opts out, the next claim skips before getting the provider's stored
     answer. Fix: the same as A in issue 2, treating uncertain messages as
     possibly delivered.
  5. The provider's delay has no upper limit. With a delay over 60 s, a retry
     could take over a key that is still being processed, and the provider would
     "send" twice. Fix: cap the delay at 30 s.
  6. Special characters in PROVIDER_DB_PASSWORD, such as @ or /, break the
     provider's database URL, and the stack won't start. Fix: document which
     characters are allowed, or pass the password separately.
  7. The e2e test only checks one direction. It checks "sent ⇒ the provider
     accepted it once", but not "the provider accepted it ⇒ it's recorded sent",
     so the bug in issue 1 would pass. Fix: check both directions, allowing
     only the "last attempt timed out" case.
  8. Two tight timing margins could make tests flaky on a slow machine: e2e case
     2 (1 s margin), and the rate test's provider-side check. Fix: widen the
     margins, and keep the provider-side rate check as a log line only; the
     worker-side check is the guarantee.

  Not judged by the reviewer:
  - Retry-After given as a date is ignored; the mock only sends seconds.
  - How fast message claiming stays on a very large messages table; not
    measured.
  - The final attempt timing out after the provider has actually accepted the
    message ends as failed, which is inherent to capped at-least-once delivery.
  - A few rare crash paths, which end truthfully.

  My recommendation: fix 1 and 3–8, each with a failing test first. For issue 2,
  choose A or B. I recommend A, and its fix also resolves issue 4.

  Should I go ahead, and which rule for issue 2?

⏺ I'll go with A and fix all eight findings, then build a full suite of
  lifecycle tests. Everything stays test-first: the tests come before the fixes,
  and I watch each one fail.

  How I'll implement A, and findings 1 and 4, with one shared mechanism: each
  message records whether its delivery is uncertain. That's the case when an
  attempt ended without a clear answer (timeout, network error, interrupted by
  shutdown), or when a worker died mid-attempt (detected when an expired lease
  is reclaimed).
  - Resend (A): excludes anyone with an uncertain, sending or sent message on
    that channel.
  - Shutdown (finding 1): an interrupted attempt is recorded as interrupted, the
    message goes straight back to the queue, and the attempt doesn't count
    toward the 5-attempt limit.
  - Skip records (finding 4): when a message whose delivery is uncertain is
    later cancelled or skipped for consent, it gets a note saying an earlier
    attempt may have reached the provider. Cancel and opt-out stay respected;
    nothing is re-sent to find out.

  This needs a small migration: a column delivery_uncertain, plus interrupted as
  an attempt outcome. Recording the decisions first, D31 for your rule A and D5
  for the uncertainty tracking:
