      57  
      58  Messages may use only `{{first_name}}`, `{{last_name}}` and `{{
          city}}` and need some fixed text; text that is over the limit e
          ven with every variable empty, and control characters other tha
          n tabs and line breaks, are rejected. Values are inserted as th
          ey are stored: a value that itself contains a variable token (e
          .g. a name saved as `{{city}}`) is expanded too, in the same wa
          y when sending and in the over-limit estimate. `channels` is `[
          "sms"]`, `["email"]` or both; `time_zone` is the browser's IANA
           zone (e.g. `Europe/Istanbul`). Edits send the `version` they w
          ere based on; a stale version or a non-draft campaign returns 4
          09. Invalid input returns 422 (with `field_errors` for rules); 
          an "unsupported" brief returns 422 with `"unsupported": true` a
          nd the AI's reason, and nothing is saved. For `POST /api/campai
          gns`, if the AI answers invalidly twice, times out or is unavai
          lable, the API returns 502/504 and nothing is saved; content ge
          neration reports the same failures as an `error` event in its s
          tream.
      59  
     ...
       94  
       95  The `worker` service starts approved campaigns when their send
            time comes, evaluating the audience at that moment (D30), and
            sends each message through the provider with its message ID a
           s `Idempotency-Key`. Before every request it re-checks that th
           e campaign is still sending and the recipient still allows the
            channel, and it personalizes and length-checks the text: over
           -limit messages are skipped (`character_limit_exceeded`) and c
           an be resent later (D31). 200 → `sent`; 429, 5xx, timeouts and
            network errors are retried with exponential backoff and jitte
           r (honouring `Retry-After`) up to `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5); other 4x
           x → `failed`. Every attempt is recorded in `delivery_attempts`
           .
       96  
       97 +A request without a clear answer (a timeout or a network error
          +) may still have been delivered, so the message is marked `del
          +ivery_uncertain`; the retry uses the same key and gets the pro
          +vider's stored result instead of a second send. The same holds
          + when a worker died mid-attempt (its expired lease is reclaime
          +d). If the campaign is cancelled or the recipient opts out bef
          +ore that retry, the message is recorded as cancelled or skippe
          +d with a note that delivery is uncertain, and a later resend l
          +eaves them out (D31). Stopping the worker interrupts in-flight
          + requests: they are recorded as `interrupted`, do not count to
          +ward `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, and the message is retried at once; messa
          +ges still waiting for a rate-limit slot go straight back to th
          +e queue.                                                      
       98 +                                                              
       99  Per channel at most 100 provider requests leave in any second,
            retries included: a rate limiter spaces them 10 ms apart and 
           a hard cap counts actual departures (D19). The cap is exact on
            the worker's side; at the provider, a request delayed by more
            than 50 ms between leaving the queue and arriving could in th
           eory still bunch with later ones.

⏺ All eight review findings are fixed with your option A, the new lifecycle
  tests are in, and everything is pushed. PR #4 is open and not merged.

  The fixes

  Finding: 1. Shutdown marked an accepted message failed
  Fix: An interrupted request is recorded as interrupted, doesn't count toward
  the 5 attempts, and is retried at once with the same key. Messages still
  waiting for a rate-limit slot go straight back to the queue instead of being
  stuck for 30 s.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: 2. A resend could send twice (option A)
  Fix: Each message now records an uncertain delivery: an attempt without a
  clear
  answer, or a worker that died mid-attempt. A resend leaves out anyone who was
   sent, is still sending, or is uncertain. D31 and D5 have dated notes.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: 3. Cancel check ran before the cap wait
  Fix: The last cancel and consent check now comes after all waiting, right
  before the request leaves.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: 4. A delivered message could be recorded as cancelled
  Fix: A cancel or opt-out is still respected, and the record now says "delivery

  uncertain: an earlier attempt may have reached the provider".
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: 5. Unbounded provider delay
  Fix: Capped at 30 s.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: 6. Special characters in the provider password
  Fix: Only URL-safe characters are allowed, with a clear error message.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: 7. The e2e test only checked one direction
  Fix: It now also checks that every message the provider really sent is
  recorded
  sent, or honestly marked uncertain.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: 8. Tight timing margins
  Fix: Widened; the provider-side rate check is now a log line, and the
  worker-side check stays strict.

  New tests (12 lifecycle tests, all against the real mock provider, plus 4 unit
  tests): timeout then retry delivered once via the stored answer; shutdown
  during the last attempt; crash after acceptance, reclaimed and delivered once;
  crash then cancel, with the note and no resend; the reviewer's duplicate
  scenario; consent revoked between retries, with and without an uncertain
  earlier attempt; renamed recipient skipped for length then resent once; cancel
  mid-campaign with no request leaving afterwards; two workers on one queue;
  retries used up; shutdown while waiting; interrupted attempts not counted. The
  unit tests cover every uncertain case in the resend rule, reclaim marking
  uncertainty, the cap-before-check order, and the config limits.

  Proof that the tests catch failures: nine deliberate bugs, one per fix, were
  all caught, each by the test meant for it.

  Bugs found along the way
  - Two of my own test mistakes: the provider log counted a request only once it
    finished, and my claim helper kept getting the same message back. Both
    fixed.
  - One older test still faked earlier attempts by setting only the claim
    counter. I updated it to record real attempts, which is how attempts are
    counted now.

  Final checks
  - make test: all 12 packages pass, twice, under full parallel load.
  - make lint and go mod tidy are clean. Each fix commit builds and vets on its
    own.
  - Live: migration 3 applied, all services healthy, and a graceful worker stop
    took 1 s with exit code 0. Your data is unchanged.

  (https://github.com/[github-user]/smart-campaign-composer/pull/4) now has 19
